Dear Clair:
894.501/8-1346
The Wall Street Journe1 of August 7, 1946 contains a
front page article by S. H. Scheibla on Corwin Edwaras' three
volume report on the Japanese Zaibatsu. The erticle states that
the report is confidential, but Scheibla's article indicates that
he hes seen it. He reproduces statistical data and quotes directly
from the report.
I wonder in view of the publicity which its contents
have received if you could not furnish us with a copy of the report.
Cordially yours,
Genge

DEAHICISIATE
My dear George:
I have reseived your letter of August 13 in which
you request a copy of the Repert of the Mission on
Japanese Combines to which reference was made In a
recent Wall Street Journal artiole. The Department
has shown Miss Seheibla, the author of this artiole,
only Part I of the Misslon's report whioh eontains
unelassiried analytical and technieal data relating to
the zaibatsu. The referenee in her artiele to the
confidential status of the report must have related to
Part II, the seotion on recommendations. Since this
section is elassified, it has not been released by the
Department.
894.501/a-1346
Part I is now being reproduced for publie distri-
bution. It is expeoted that it will be available about
September 1 at whieh time the Department will be glad
to send you a copy. If, however, it is urgent that you
see the report prior to that date I shall be glad to
arrange for you or members of your staff to review our
file copy in the Department.
Sincerely yours,

SUBJECT: Request for Materials From Japan
DK
The Researoh and Statistios Division, Boonomio and Soiontifio
Section, SCAP, Tokyo, has under way a comprehensive economio research
program on Japan. It has been brought to our attention that the above
named Division has copies of their reports available for distribution,
but that transmittal would have to depend upon offioial requests. Spe-
oifically DRF requests the following:
894.5
(1) Two copies of all research studies inoluding special re-
ports, produoed by the Researoh and Statistios Division.
(2) Two copies of the Monthly Bulletin of Japanese Statistios
and all other statistioal series on Japanese production, consumption, im-
ports, and exports of different industries, dating baok to the 1930's.
(3) A copy of all roporting forms which have been adopted by
the Researoh and Statistics Division for the use of the Japanese, together
with any explanations whioh have been prepared to acoompany these forms.
-1046
(4) Copies of data on plant capacity and maohine tool holdings
referred to on pages 6 and 7 of the Progress Roport of the Researoh and
Statistios Division for June 20, 1946.
(5) A copy of the consus of industry referred to on page 8 of
the above report.
It is suggested that this request be transmitted to the Department
of State Mission in Tokyo by the most expeditious means. This request is
urgent in the light of the Japan Brench's presont series of studies on
FW894.501/10-
Japanese reparations (0CL 4126 - 4146 inolusive, 3771, 3772). None of the
above listed itoms are available in Washington for our retention.

Tokyo's No. 683
November 4, 1946
-3-
preparation but will not be ready for distribution until some
time after January 1. It will be forwarded as soon as re-
ceived.
It is regretted that it has not been possible to prepare
any analysis or summary of this material and, in this connec-
tion, reference is respectfully made to our requests for at
least one officer to handle the informational work of this
Mission. Only the splendid cooperation of General Headquarters,
Supreme Commander for the Allied Powers, has made possible the
assembling of this extensive data which has nevertheless been
done at the expense of sacrificing attention to other daily
pressing work and with the expenditure of considerable over-
time on the part of our staff.
Respectfully yours,
George Atcheson, Jr.

=== Page 117 === (confidence 0.97)
FINANCE
FINANCE
AND CLB ACCOUETS OF BANKS
Pinancial reorganization regulting from such measures as
seizure of property for reparations, the special tax on war damage
claime, etc. will necessitate a rovaluation of many of the assets
and liabilities of Japanese banks. In order to parait the banks to
continue operations wile this revaluation is in process, the ac-
counts of all banks were split into two parts on August 11, 16:
(1) New Accounts, representing in general those accounts which did
not require revaluation; and (2) Cld Accounts, representing those
which may have to be readjusted.
Assets originally transforred to the New Accommt consisted
primarily of casn, deposite in the Bask of Japan, deposits in and
loens to financial institutions, and Mational Governmant and dome-
tic municipal bonds.
Liabilities griginally transferred to the New Account com-
sisted primarily of: (1) Pree deposits (that is, deposits of the
Governpent and financial imetitutions and those made by individuals
and corporatioms in free yem); (2) Pirst Restricted Deposite com-
puted as followe: (a) all deposit accounts of less than 3,000m;
(b) for individual deposits, a maximm of 15,000 yen per household
or 4,000 yan per momber of the household up to a maximum of 32,000
yen for the household; (c) for corporations a maximum of 15,000
yen. (9) Borrowing from the Bank of Japan and other financial i-
stitutione.
Other assets and liabilities were placed in the Old Account.
When the assets and liabilitien of the Old Account are readjusted
they are to bo transferred to the New Aocount.
